\begin{theorem}\label{r3:thm-zero}
There is exactly one zero $x^*=(g^*,c^*)$ of $\Fc$ in $\overline B_{\mathcal X}(x^\circ,10^{-7})$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The finite dyadic product and the Toeplitz symbol bounds give an injective $A$ by Lemmas~\ref{r3:lem-principal} and~\ref{r3:lem-A}. The partition above covers all derivative columns and yields $Z<0.776701$. The verifier evaluates both inequalities \eqref{r3:eq-radii} with the full Arb enclosures of $Y,Z,\nrm A,c_2,c_3,c_4$ at the exact radius $10^{-7}$. Their left sides are respectively below $-9.705\cdot10^{-9}$ and $0.8421<1$, so Proposition~\ref{r3:prop-NK} applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Shape scale]\label{r3:rem-centre}
The exact $b$ is held fixed while the shape varies. At the same centre, the scale $\sigma=3b$ cannot certify this fixed-point operator: the normalised defect of shape column $j=183$ is strictly greater than $1.15486483728$, as recorded in \url{anc/r3_convex/SCALE3_OBSTRUCTION.json}. This excludes contraction for that operator in that norm; it says nothing about other operators or centres. The scale $8b$ used above gives $Z<0.776701$ and $c_2<3.163938$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Reconstruction and convexity}\label{r3:sec-recon}
Let $\psi=\psi_{c^*}$, and put $\varepsilon=\nrm{c^*-c^\circ}_{\mathcal E}\leq r/\sigma$. The existence ball projects inside the shape ball of $\mathcal E$-radius one; in fact the projected radius is $68719476736/595786147695156171875<1$. Write
\[
D_1=\sum_{j\geq3}j|c_j^\circ|,\quad D_2=\sum_{j\geq3}j(j-1)|c_j^\circ|,\quad H=\sup_{j\geq3,\ j\,\mathrm{odd}}(j-1)R^{-j}.
\]
The last supremum is a finite maximum through $j=21$, since the ratio of successive odd-index terms is at most one for $j\geq21$.

\begin{proposition}\label{r3:prop-geometry}
For every $c$ with $\nrm{c-c^\circ}_{\mathcal E}\leq1$, the map $\psi_c$ is univalent on $|w|\leq101/100$, its image $D=\psi_c(\D)$ is strictly convex and strictly star-shaped, and $c_{19}\ne0$. More precisely, on the closed unit disc,
\begin{equation}\label{r3:eq-geom}
\re\left(1+\frac{w\psi_c''(w)}{\psi_c'(w)}\right)>0.7878,
\end{equation}
and $\re\psi_c'>106.2027$ on $|w|\leq101/100$, $\re(w\psi_c'/\psi_c)>0.9717$ on $|w|=1$, and $|c_{19}|>0.000665$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For $|w|\leq1$ the triangle inequality gives
\[
\re\left(1+\frac{w\psi_c''}{\psi_c'}\right)
\geq1-\frac{D_2+\varepsilon H}{c_1^\circ-D_1-\varepsilon/R}.
\]
The denominator is positive, and the interval computation gives a lower bound $>0.7878989239$ at the worst case $\varepsilon=1$. The same coefficient inequalities on the larger disc give $\re\psi_c'>106.2027$ there. Integration along line segments proves univalence; vertical segments give $q_c=\im\psi_c/y>0$. The separate boundary estimate gives the stated strict star-shapedness, and the weighted coefficient bound gives $|c_{19}|\geq|c_{19}^\circ|-\varepsilon/(19R^{19})>0.000665$.

For $\gamma(\theta)=\psi_c(e^{i\theta})$, its tangent angle derivative is $\re(1+w\psi_c''/\psi_c')$, which is positive by \eqref{r3:eq-geom}. As the simple tangent winds once, every oriented tangent line supports the interior, so $D$ is strictly convex. It is symmetric under reflection in both axes because $\psi_c$ is odd with real coefficients. The lift $\Theta_c(B)$ is convex exactly when $D$ is: one direction follows by intersecting with a meridian plane; conversely, the vertical sections of $D$ are symmetric intervals, and for $X,Y$ in the solid, convexity of $D$ and $|tX'+(1-t)Y'|\leq t|X'|+(1-t)|Y'|$ show that $tX+(1-t)Y$ lies in the solid for $0\leq t\leq1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{r3:lem-regularity}
Let $n\ge2$ and let $\Omega\subset\R^n$ be a bounded domain with real-analytic boundary, and let $u\in C^1(\overline\Omega)$ satisfy $\int_\Omega(\nabla u\cdot\nabla\varphi-u\varphi)=0$ for all $\varphi\in C_c^\infty(\Omega)$ and $u=1$ on $\partial\Omega$. Then $u$ extends to a real-analytic function on an open neighbourhood of $\overline\Omega$ which satisfies $\Delta u+u=0$ there.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $v=u-1$. For $\varepsilon>0$ the function $\max(|v|-\varepsilon,0)\operatorname{sign}v$ is Lipschitz with compact support in $\Omega$ and converges to $v$ in $W^{1,2}(\Omega)$ as $\varepsilon\to0$, so $v\in W^{1,2}_0(\Omega)$; it is a weak solution of $\Delta v+v=-1$. By global regularity for the Dirichlet problem \cite[Theorem~8.13]{GT01}, applied for every $k$, $v\in W^{k,2}(\Omega)$ for all $k$, hence $v\in C^\infty(\overline\Omega)$. By the theorem of Morrey and Nirenberg on analyticity up to the boundary \cite{MN57} (constant coefficients, analytic right-hand side, analytic boundary and zero Dirichlet data), every point of $\partial\Omega$ has a ball around it to which $v|_\Omega$ extends real-analytically; the extension solves $\Delta v+v=-1$ by the identity theorem. Interior analyticity is classical (see also Lemma~\ref{lem:interior}). The local extensions are glued to an extension on a neighbourhood of $\overline\Omega$ exactly as in the last step of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:boundary}.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:r3}}
The estimates of Proposition~\ref{r3:prop-geometry} hold for $c^*$ because $r/\sigma<1$. The proofs of Lemmas~\ref{r3:lem-lift} and~\ref{r3:lem-pullback} apply on the smaller disc $|w|<101/100$, a neighbourhood of the closed unit disc; they give an analytic diffeomorphism $\Theta_{c^*}$ near $\overline B$, with Jacobian $q_{c^*}|p_{c^*}|^2>0$. Let $\Omega=\Theta_{c^*}(B)$, $U=1+\iota(K_3g^*)$, and $u=U\circ\Theta_{c^*}^{-1}$. Since $\Fc(x^*)=0$, Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-F} yields the pulled-back weak Helmholtz equation and zero Cauchy traces of $U-1$; Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-pullback} transfers these to $u$. The analytic lift across the rotation axis is valid because $\psi$ has odd real coefficients and $q=\im\psi/y$ is analytic in $(x_1,y^2)$. Interior analyticity also follows by harmonic lifting or elliptic regularity, and Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-regularity} extends $u$ analytically across the boundary.

The positive boundary bound makes $\Omega$ strictly star-shaped. The solid-of-revolution argument in Proposition~\ref{r3:prop-geometry} makes it convex. The lift of the sphere is a compact real-analytic surface diffeomorphic to $S^2$ and has the stated $O(2)\times\mathbf Z_2$ invariance. A constant solution of $\Delta u+u=0$ would be zero, contradicting $u=1$ on the boundary. If $\Omega$ were a ball, its symmetries would put its centre at zero, and $D$ would be a disc centred at zero. Schwarz's lemma applied to the normalised conformal map and its inverse would force $\psi$ to be linear, contrary to $c_{19}\ne0$. This proves the theorem.

\subsection{An additional nonconvex example}\label{r3:sec-AX3}
The earlier three-dimensional construction is independent of the convex one and remains useful as a second exact branch.
\begin{proposition}\label{r3:prop-AX3}
There is a bounded, strictly star-shaped, non-ball domain $\Omega_{\mathrm{old}}\subset\R^3$ with real-analytic boundary diffeomorphic to $S^2$ and a nonconstant analytic solution of \eqref{eq:main}. It has the same $O(2)\times\mathbf Z_2$ symmetry, but is not convex: both principal curvatures at the two poles, with respect to the inner normal, are negative.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Apply the preceding analytic lemmas and the contraction criterion with the independent centre of the nonconvex example and its parameters $M=180$, $S=64$, $R=21/20$, $b_{\mathrm{old}}=\texttt{0x1.85067a9afca2dp+5}$, $\sigma=3b_{\mathrm{old}}$ and $r=3\cdot10^{-7}$. Its complete verifier and two certificate files are in \texttt{anc/r3/}; the certified bounds are displayed in Table~\ref{r3:tab-old}. The corresponding radius polynomial is $<-4.81\cdot10^{-9}$ and contraction bound is $<0.959201$. Its geometry certificate gives $\re(w\psi'/\psi)>0.83$ on the unit circle and a nonzero $c_{13}$, so the same reconstruction gives an analytic, strictly star-shaped non-ball domain and solution. Nonconvexity follows from Proposition~\ref{r3:prop-nonconvex}.
\end{proof}
\begin{table}[ht]
\centering\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{@{}ccccccc@{}}
\toprule
$(M,S)$ & $\sigma$ & $\nrm A$ & $Y$ & $Z$ & $r$ & Geometry\\
\midrule
$(180,64)$ & $3b_{\mathrm{old}}$ & $<5849.52$ & $<2.10029\cdot10^{-8}$ & $<0.858929$ & $3\cdot10^{-7}$ & nonconvex at poles\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{The independent nonconvex certificate; see \texttt{anc/r3/certificate\_r3.json}.}
\label{r3:tab-old}
\end{table}

\begin{proposition}\label{r3:prop-nonconvex}
For the centre of the nonconvex example $c^{\mathrm{old},\circ}$, exact rational arithmetic gives
\[
42.81903<\sum_jjc_j^{\mathrm{old},\circ}<42.81904,\qquad
-7.758015<\sum_jj^2c_j^{\mathrm{old},\circ}<-7.758014.
\]
Throughout its certified existence ball,
$1+\psi''(1)/\psi'(1)<-0.181$; hence both principal curvatures at each pole are negative.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The shape perturbation has $\mathcal E$ norm at most $r/(3b_{\mathrm{old}})<2.06\cdot10^{-9}$. Its effect on $\psi'(1)$ is at most $e/R$, and on $\psi'(1)+\psi''(1)$ at most $\sup_{t>0}tR^{-t}\,e<7.55e<1.6\cdot10^{-8}$. Thus $0<\psi'(1)<42.82$ and $\psi'(1)+\psi''(1)<-7.758$, giving the claimed ratio. The meridian curvature at a pole, with inward normal, is $(1+\psi''(1)/\psi'(1))/\psi'(1)<0$; rotation symmetry makes both surface principal curvatures equal to it. The other pole follows by reflection. The exact dyadic check is \texttt{anc/r3/check\_nonconvex\_r3.py}.
\end{proof}


%======================================================================
\section{Dimension five: weighted spaces}\label{r5:sec}\label{sec:r5}
%======================================================================

In this section we prove Theorem~\ref{thm:main} for $n=5$. The construction follows Section~\ref{sec:r3}: the problem is pulled back to the unit ball $B\subset\R^5$ by the axisymmetric extension of a conformal map of the meridian section, the unknown field is written as $1+K_5g$, and the existence of a zero of a quartic polynomial map is proved by a contraction argument around an exact dyadic centre. The new ingredient is functional-analytic. For $n\geq5$ the Jacobi coefficient norm of Section~\ref{sec:r3} does not control $K_ng$ in $C^1$ (Remark~\ref{r5:rem-obstruction}), so that a zero in that norm would not yield a function with the two boundary traces. We therefore grade all coefficient norms by the weight
\[
\Upsilon(D)=1+\frac D{10^6}
\]
of the polynomial degree $D$. The $C^1$ bound then holds with a large but finite constant, which enters only the reconstruction, while the multiplication constants are those of the unweighted norms and all other operator bounds acquire at most factors $\Upsilon(\Delta)$ with $\Delta\leq1000$, that is, at most $1.001$.

\emph{Notation.} Points of the physical space are $X=(X_1,X')\in\R\times\R^4$ and points of the reference space are $x=(x_1,x')\in\R\times\R^4$. The meridian variables $y=|x'|$, $w=x_1+iy$, $r=|w|=|x|$, $t=r^2$, $\xi=x_1/r$, the constant $R=21/20$, the polynomials $T_n$, $U_n$, $C^{(2)}_n$ and $J^\beta_s$, the map $\psi_c(w)=\sum_{j\ \mathrm{odd}}c_jw^j$ and the functions $p_c=\psi_c'$ and $q_c=\im\psi_c/y$, with the expansions \eqref{r3:eq-qseries} and \eqref{r3:eq-p2}, are as in Sections~\ref{r3:sec-pullback} and~\ref{r3:sec-spaces}; they involve only the meridian variables. Now $B$ is the unit ball of $\R^5$, and $\zeta=R^{-2}$. The objects $H_\ell$, $\Psi_{\ell,s}$, $D_{n\ell}$, $h_\ell$, $C_{\ell n}$, $\mathcal T_n$, $\mathcal L$, $\Gc$, $\mathcal Y$, $\mathcal E$, $\mathcal X$, $\tau$, $\nrm\cdot_{\mathrm{ch}}$, $\Fc$ and $A$ of Section~\ref{sec:r3} are redefined below for $n=5$; we keep the letters.

%----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{The pull-back and the obstruction}\label{r5:sec-pullback}
%----------------------------------------------------------------------

For $c=(c_j)_{j\ \mathrm{odd}}$ with $\sum_jjR^j|c_j|<\infty$ let $\Theta_c(x)=\big(\re\psi_c(x_1+i|x'|),\,q_c(x)\,x'\big)$, now with $x'\in\R^4$.

\begin{lemma}\label{r5:lem-lift}
Let $c$ be as above with $\re\psi_c'>0$ on $\{|w|<R_0\}$ for some $1<R_0<R$, and let $\rho>0$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item The functions $q_c$, $\partial_{x_1}q_c$, $y^{-1}\partial_yq_c$, $|p_c|^2$ and $\re\psi_c(x_1+iy)$ are real analytic on $B_{R_0}=\{x\in\R^5:|x|<R_0\}$, and $q_c>0$ there. The map $\Theta_c$ is a real-analytic diffeomorphism of $B_{R_0}$ onto an open subset of $\R^5$, commuting with the action of $O(4)$ on $x'$ and with $x_1\mapsto-x_1$; $\det D\Theta_c=q_c^3|p_c|^2$; and $(\det D\Theta_c)(D\Theta_c^{\mathsf T}D\Theta_c)^{-1}\nabla U=q_c^3\nabla U$ for every axisymmetric $C^1$ function $U$.
\item Let $U\in C^1(\overline B)$ be axisymmetric and satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{r5:eq-weak}
\int_Bq_c^3\big(\nabla U\cdot\nabla\varphi-\rho^2|p_c|^2U\varphi\big)\,dx=0\qquad(\varphi\in C^\infty_c(B)).
\end{equation}
Then $u=U\circ\Theta_c^{-1}$ satisfies $\int_{\Theta_c(B)}(\nabla u\cdot\nabla\varphi-\rho^2u\varphi)\,dX=0$ for all $\varphi\in C^\infty_c(\Theta_c(B))$, and if $U=1$ and $\nabla U=0$ on $\partial B$, then $u=1$ and $\nabla u=0$ on $\partial\Theta_c(B)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proofs of Lemmas~\ref{r3:lem-lift} and~\ref{r3:lem-pullback} apply with $x'\in\R^4$. At a point with $x'\neq0$ let $e_\varrho=x'/|x'|$ and let $e_{\phi,1},e_{\phi,2},e_{\phi,3}$ be an orthonormal basis of the orthogonal complement of $e_\varrho$ in $\R^4$. On the half-plane spanned by $e_1$ and $e_\varrho$, $\Theta_c$ acts as $\psi_c$, and $D\Theta_c$ is $|p_c|$ times a rotation there; $\Theta_c$ maps the $3$-sphere $\{(x_1,z'):|z'|=y\}$ onto the $3$-sphere of radius $q_cy$ in the hyperplane $\{X_1=\re\psi_c(x_1+iy)\}$ by $z'\mapsto q_cz'$, so that $D\Theta_ce_{\phi,i}=q_ce_{\phi,i}$. Hence $D\Theta_c^{\mathsf T}D\Theta_c=\mathrm{diag}(|p_c|^2,|p_c|^2,q_c^2,q_c^2,q_c^2)$ in this frame, which gives the determinant, and, since $\nabla U$ lies in the span of $e_1$ and $e_\varrho$, the identity for $\nabla U$. This identity is pointwise, so in the change of variables of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-pullback} the test functions $\varphi\circ\Theta_c$ need not be axisymmetric.
\end{proof}

For an axisymmetric $U=1+W$ with $W$ of class $C^2$ one has, off the axis, $\nabla q_c\cdot\nabla W=\partial_{x_1}q_c\,\partial_{x_1}W+(y^{-1}\partial_yq_c)\,y\partial_yW$ and hence
\begin{equation}\label{r5:eq-div}
\nabla\cdot(q_c^3\nabla U)+\rho^2q_c^3|p_c|^2U=q_c^2\Big(q_c\Delta W+3\partial_{x_1}q_c\,\partial_{x_1}W+3\frac{\partial_yq_c}y\,y\partial_yW+\rho^2q_c|p_c|^2U\Big).
\end{equation}
The factor $q_c^{n-3}=q_c^2$ is positive, and the bracket is linear in $q_c$, $\partial_{x_1}q_c$, $y^{-1}\partial_yq_c$, $q_c|p_c|^2$ and in $W$. With $\Delta W=g$ it is a polynomial of degree four in the field and shape coefficients, and no division by the unknown $q_c$ occurs. (For $n=3$ the same computation gives \eqref{r3:eq-F}; in $\R^n$ the exponents are $n-2$ and $n-3$.)

\begin{remark}[Why a weight is needed]\label{r5:rem-obstruction}
Let $n\geq3$, $\lambda=(n-2)/2$, and let $K_n$ act on $\Psi_{0,s}=J^\lambda_s(t)$ by the three-term formula \eqref{r3:eq-K} with $d=\lambda+2s$ (for $n=3$ this is $K_3$, for $n=5$ it is \eqref{r5:eq-K} below). By Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-K}(a), which holds for every $\beta$, $\partial_rK_n\Psi_{0,s}=\frac r{2(d+1)}\big(J^{\lambda+1}_s(t)-J^{\lambda+1}_{s-1}(t)\big)$, and $J^\beta_s(t)=(-1)^sP^{(\beta,0)}_s(1-2t)$. By the Mehler--Heine formula \cite[18.11.5]{DLMF}, which holds uniformly for bounded arguments, for every fixed $z>0$
\[
\lim_{s\to\infty}s^{1-\lambda}\,\Big|\partial_rK_n\Psi_{0,s}\Big|_{r=z/(2s)}=\frac12\Big(\frac z2\Big)^{-\lambda}|J_{\lambda+1}(z)| .
\]
Thus $\sup|\nabla K_n\Psi_{0,s}|$ is at least of order $s^{(n-4)/2}$, while the norm of $\Psi_{0,s}$ in the space $\mathcal L$ of Section~\ref{sec:r3} (and in its analogue for $\R^n$) is $h_0=1$ for all $s$. For $n\geq5$ there is therefore no estimate $\nrm{K_ng}_{C^1}\leq C\nrm g_{\mathcal L}$, and the reconstruction step of Section~\ref{r3:sec-recon} fails in these norms. (For $n=3$ the growth is $s^{-1/2}$, in accordance with Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-C1}.) The growth is polynomial in the degree, and the weight $(1+D/c)^{(n-3)/2}$ suffices (Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-C1} and the remark after it); for $n=5$ this is $1+D/c$, and we take $c=10^6$.
\end{remark}

%----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Graded coefficient spaces}\label{r5:sec-spaces}
%----------------------------------------------------------------------

For $\ell\geq0$ let $Y_\ell=C^{(3/2)}_\ell/C^{(3/2)}_\ell(1)$, where $C^{(3/2)}_\ell(1)=(\ell+1)(\ell+2)/2$, and let $H_\ell(x)=r^\ell Y_\ell(\xi)$, the axisymmetric harmonic polynomial on $\R^5$, homogeneous of degree $\ell$, with $H_\ell(1,0)=1$; $|H_\ell|\leq1$ on $\overline B$. With $\lambda^+_\ell=(\ell+3)/(2\ell+3)$, $\lambda^-_\ell=\ell/(2\ell+3)$ and $H_{-1}=0$,
\begin{equation}\label{r5:eq-Hrec}
\begin{gathered}
\xi Y_\ell=\lambda^+_\ell Y_{\ell+1}+\lambda^-_\ell Y_{\ell-1},\qquad x_1H_\ell=\lambda^+_\ell H_{\ell+1}+\lambda^-_\ell\,tH_{\ell-1},\\
\partial_{x_1}H_\ell=\ell H_{\ell-1},\qquad(x\cdot\nabla)H_\ell=\ell H_\ell,
\end{gathered}
\end{equation}
by the three-term recurrence of $C^{(3/2)}_\ell$ and the argument after \eqref{r3:eq-Hrec}. Let $\beta_\ell=\ell+\frac32$ and $\Psi_{\ell,s}=H_\ell J^{\beta_\ell}_s(t)$, of degree $\ell+2s$. Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-jacobi} holds for every $\beta>0$ and is used without change, and $\Delta(H_\ell v(t))=4H_\ell\big(tv''+(\beta_\ell+1)v'\big)$ in $\R^5$, by \eqref{r5:eq-Hrec} and $\Delta v(|x|^2)=4tv''+10v'$. In spherical coordinates, Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-jacobi}(a) and the Fischer decomposition show as in Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-embed} that the $\Psi_{\ell,s}$ are pairwise orthogonal in $L^2(B)$, that $\Psi_{\ell,s}$ is orthogonal to all polynomials of degree less than $\ell+2s$, and that $\nrm{\Psi_{\ell,s}}^2_{L^2(B)}=|S^4|/(2\nu_\ell(\ell+2s+\frac52))$, where $\nu_\ell=(2\ell+3)(\ell+1)(\ell+2)/6$ is the dimension of the spherical harmonics of degree $\ell$ on $S^4$.

Let $\mathsf a_k=(\frac32)_k/k!$, a nondecreasing sequence with $\sum_k\mathsf a_k\zeta^k=(1-\zeta)^{-3/2}$. Factoring $(1-2\xi z+z^2)^{-3/2}$ as in Section~\ref{r3:sec-spaces} gives $Y_\ell=\sum_nD_{n\ell}T_n$ with
\[
D_{\ell-2k,\ell}=(2-\delta_{\ell,2k})\,\mathsf a_k\mathsf a_{\ell-k}/C^{(3/2)}_\ell(1)\geq0,
\]
and we put $h_\ell=\sum_nD_{n\ell}R^n\geq Y_\ell(1)=1$. We write $T_K=\sum_\ell C_{\ell K}Y_\ell$ and $\mathcal T_K=\sum_\ell C_{\ell K}H_\ell$, the harmonic polynomial with boundary values $T_K(\xi)$; $|\mathcal T_K|\leq1$ on $\overline B$.

\subsubsection*{Graded norms}
The weight $\Upsilon(D)=1+D/10^6$ is nondecreasing and submultiplicative, $\Upsilon(a+b)\leq\Upsilon(a)\Upsilon(b)$. The spaces $\mathcal L$, $\Gc$, $\mathcal Y$, $\mathcal E$ and $\mathcal X=\Gc\times\mathcal E$ are defined as in Section~\ref{r3:sec-spaces}, with the norms
\begin{gather*}
\nrm f_{\mathcal L}=\sum_{\ell,s\geq0}h_\ell\Upsilon(\ell+2s)|f_{\ell,s}|,\qquad\nrm f_{\mathcal Y}=\sum_nR^n\Upsilon(n)|f_n|+\sum_{\ell,\,s\geq1}h_\ell\Upsilon(\ell+2s)|f_{\ell,s}|,\\
\nrm c_{\mathcal E}=\sum_{j\ \mathrm{odd}}jR^j\Upsilon(j-1)|c_j|,\qquad\nrm{(g,c)}_{\mathcal X}=\nrm g_{\mathcal L}+\sigma\nrm c_{\mathcal E},
\end{gather*}
where $f\in\mathcal Y$ is identified with $\sum_nf_n\mathcal T_n+\sum_{s\geq1}f_{\ell,s}\Psi_{\ell,s}$ and $\sigma$ is fixed in Section~\ref{r5:sec-quartic}. Thus every basis element carries its weight from Section~\ref{sec:r3} times $\Upsilon$ of its polynomial degree: $\ell+2s$ for $\Psi_{\ell,s}$, $n$ for $\mathcal T_n$, and $j-1=\deg jw^{j-1}$ for the shape mode $w^j$. Since $\sum_{m\leq\ell}D_{m\ell}R^m\Upsilon(m)\leq h_\ell\Upsilon(\ell)$, the inclusion $\mathcal L^{\mathrm{ev}}\subset\mathcal Y$ is norm-decreasing. For polynomials and series $m=\sum m_{a,k}H_at^k=\sum\tilde m_{n,k}r^{n+2k}T_n(\xi)$ we put
\[
\tau(m)=\sum_{a,k}h_a\Upsilon(a+2k)|m_{a,k}|,\qquad\nrm m_{\mathrm{ch}}=\sum_{n,k}R^n\Upsilon(n+2k)|\tilde m_{n,k}| .
\]
All layers are graded by the same function of the degree. Every product occurring below raises the degree by at most the degree of the multiplier, and the graded shape norm pays exactly for the degree of the multipliers it produces; if only the field layer were graded, the columns of the linearisation at high shape modes would lose their decay.

\begin{lemma}\label{r5:lem-conn}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item $C_{00}=1$, and for $K\geq1$ and $0\leq i\leq K/2$,
\[
C_{K-2i,K}=\frac K2\cdot\frac{(-\frac32)_i\,(K-i-1)!}{i!\,(\frac52)_{K-i}}\cdot\frac{K-2i+\frac32}{\frac32}\cdot C^{(3/2)}_{K-2i}(1).
\]
\item Let $e_i=|(-\frac32)_i|/i!$ and $\mathsf w_i=e_i\zeta^i(1-\zeta)^{-3/2}$. Then $|C_{K-2i,K}|\,h_{K-2i}\leq\mathsf w_iR^K$, and $\sum_i\mathsf w_i=\kappa_5:=1+3\zeta(1-\zeta)^{-3/2}\approx96.99$.
\item $\lambda^+_\ell h_{\ell+1}+\lambda^-_\ell h_{\ell-1}\leq Rh_\ell$, and $D_{\ell-2k,\ell}R^{\ell-2k}\leq\mathsf a_k\zeta^kh_\ell$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) Divide the connection formula \cite[18.18.16]{DLMF} for $C^{(\mu)}_K$ in terms of the $C^{(3/2)}_{K-2i}$ by $\mu$ and let $\mu\to0$, using $C^{(\mu)}_K/\mu\to(2/K)T_K$ \cite[18.7.25]{DLMF} and $(\mu)_{K-i}/\mu\to(K-i-1)!$; this is the argument of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-connection}(a). (Alternatively, both sides satisfy the recurrence in $K$ that follows from $T_{K+1}=2\xi T_K-T_{K-1}$ and \eqref{r5:eq-Hrec}.)

(b) Let $\ell=K-2i$. For $\ell\geq1$, $D_{\ell\ell}=2\mathsf a_\ell/C^{(3/2)}_\ell(1)$, and (a) gives $|C_{\ell K}|D_{\ell\ell}=e_i\varrho_i$ with $\varrho_0=1$ and, for $i\geq1$,
\[
\varrho_i=\frac K{K-i+\frac32}\prod_{r=1}^{i-1}\frac{\ell+r}{\ell+r+\frac32}\leq\frac{K(\ell+1)}{(K-i+\frac32)(\ell+i)}\leq1,
\]
by telescoping and $(K-i+\frac32)(\ell+i)-K(\ell+1)=i^2-\frac32i+\frac K2>0$. For $\ell=0$ the same holds with $D_{00}=1$ and an extra factor $\frac12$. Since $\mathsf a_k$ is nondecreasing, $h_\ell\leq D_{\ell\ell}R^\ell\sum_k\mathsf a_k\zeta^k=D_{\ell\ell}R^\ell(1-\zeta)^{-3/2}$, hence $|C_{\ell K}|h_\ell\leq e_iR^{K-2i}(1-\zeta)^{-3/2}=\mathsf w_iR^K$. The numbers $e_i\zeta^i$ are the moduli of the Taylor coefficients of $(1-\zeta)^{3/2}$, of which only the coefficient $-\frac32$ of $\zeta$ is negative; so $\sum_ie_i\zeta^i=(1-\zeta)^{3/2}+3\zeta$.

(c) Apply the linear functional $\langle\cdot\rangle_R$ of Section~\ref{r3:sec-spaces} to the first identity in \eqref{r5:eq-Hrec}. The right-hand side gives $\lambda^+_\ell h_{\ell+1}+\lambda^-_\ell h_{\ell-1}$, and the left-hand side is $\sum_nD_{n\ell}(R^{n+1}+R^{|n-1|})/2\leq Rh_\ell$, because all $D_{n\ell}$ are nonnegative. The second bound follows from $D_{\ell-2k,\ell}\leq\mathsf a_kD_{\ell\ell}$ and $h_\ell\geq D_{\ell\ell}R^\ell$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{The three basic theorems}
The following three results make the scheme of Section~\ref{sec:r3} work in dimension five: a $C^1$ bound for the compatible inverse of the Laplacian, a graded multiplier algebra, and the transfer of the operator bounds to the graded norms.

For $\ell\geq0$ and $s\geq1$ let $d=\beta_\ell+2s=\ell+2s+\frac32$ and
\begin{equation}\label{r5:eq-K}
K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}=\frac{\Psi_{\ell,s-1}}{4d(d+1)}-\frac{\Psi_{\ell,s}}{2d(d+2)}+\frac{\Psi_{\ell,s+1}}{4(d+1)(d+2)}=H_\ell\,k_{\ell,s}(t).
\end{equation}

\begin{theorem}[$C^1$ bound]\label{r5:thm-C1}
For $\ell\geq0$ and $s\geq1$, $\Delta K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}=\Psi_{\ell,s}$, the function $K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}$ and its gradient vanish on $\partial B$, and, with $D=\ell+2s$,
\[
\max\Big(\sup_{\overline B}|K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}|,\ \sup_{\overline B}|\nabla K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}|\Big)\leq\frac{D+5}4\leq1.25\cdot10^6\,h_\ell\Upsilon(D).
\]
Consequently, for $g\in\Gc$ the series $W=\sum g_{\ell,s}K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}$ converges in $C^1(\overline B)$, the functions $|W|$ and $|\nabla W|$ are bounded on $\overline B$ by $1.25\cdot10^6\nrm g_{\mathcal L}$, $W=0$ and $\nabla W=0$ on $\partial B$, and $\Delta W=\iota(g)$ in $\mathcal D'(B)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first assertions are Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-K}(b),(c); their proofs use only Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-jacobi}, which holds for every $\beta$, and the formula for $\Delta(H_\ell v(t))$ above. For the bound we follow the proof of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-C1}, with $S^6\subset\R^5\times\R^2$ in place of $S^4\subset\R^3\times\R^2$. The parametrisation $(x,\phi)\mapsto(x,\sqrt{1-|x|^2}(\cos\phi,\sin\phi))$ again gives $\int_{S^6}f(x)\,d\varsigma=2\pi\int_Bf\,dx$; the dimension of the spherical harmonics of degree $m$ on $S^6$ is $N_m=(m+1)(m+2)(m+3)(m+4)(2m+5)/120$, and $|S^6|=\frac{2\pi}5|S^4|$. Part~(a) of that lemma becomes $|P(x)|^2\leq5N_m|S^4|^{-1}\nrm P^2_{L^2(B)}$ for $P\in V_m$ and $x\in\overline B$. As there, $\partial_iK_5\Psi_{\ell,s}\in V_{D-1}\oplus V_{D+1}$ and $\nrm{\nabla K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}}^2_{L^2}=\nrm{\Psi_{\ell,s}}^2_{L^2}/(2d(d+2))$, and $D+\frac52=d+1$, so that
\[
|\nabla K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}(x)|^2\leq\frac{5(N_{D-1}+N_{D+1})}{4\nu_\ell\,d(d+1)(d+2)}\leq\frac{(D+2)(D+3)(D+4)(D+5)}{24(D+\frac32)(D+\frac52)}\leq\frac{(D+5)^2}{16},
\]
using $\nu_\ell\geq1$, $N_{D-1}\leq N_{D+1}$, $2(d+2)=2D+7$ and, in the last step, $3(D+\frac32)(D+\frac52)(D+5)-2(D+2)(D+3)(D+4)=D^3+9D^2+\frac{77}4D+\frac{33}4>0$. Integration along a radius bounds $|K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}|$ by the same number. Finally $(D+5)/4\leq\frac54\cdot10^6\,\Upsilon(D)$ and $h_\ell\geq1$. The consequences follow as in Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-C1}(c), with the embedding $\iota$ of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-embed}, which is continuous because $\nrm{\Psi_{\ell,s}}_{L^2}\leq|S^4|^{1/2}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The same argument with $S^{n+1}\subset\R^n\times\R^2$ bounds the $C^1$ norm of $K_n\Psi_{\ell,s}$ in $\R^n$ by a constant times $D^{(n-3)/2}$, so the weights $(1+D/c)^{(n-3)/2}$ give a $C^1$ bound in every dimension $n\geq4$; by Remark~\ref{r5:rem-obstruction} the exponent cannot be smaller than $(n-4)/2$. The constant in Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-C1} is used only qualitatively, in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:main} for $n=5$ at the end of this section; it never enters the contraction argument, which is why $c=10^6$ may be taken so large that the weights are nearly flat on all indices that enter the computation.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Graded multiplier algebra]\label{r5:thm-mult}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item For $a,\ell\geq0$, $Y_aY_\ell=\sum_vG_{a\ell v}Y_v$ with $G_{a\ell v}\geq0$, supported on $|a-\ell|\leq v\leq a+\ell$, $v\equiv a+\ell\pmod2$, with $\sum_vG_{a\ell v}=1$ and $\sum_vG_{a\ell v}h_v\leq h_ah_\ell$.
\item For a solid monomial $H_at^k$ of degree $d=a+2k$ and $\ell,s\geq0$, the expansion \eqref{r3:eq-monomial} holds with probability vectors $\pi^{(v)}$, and Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-radial} holds with $\vartheta_d(\ell,s)=\big(d/(\ell+d+\frac72)\big)^s$ for $d\geq s$ and $\vartheta_d(\ell,s)=0$ for $d<s$.
\item For all $m$, $m_1$, $m_2$ with finite norms and $f\in\mathcal L$: $\nrm{mf}_{\mathcal L}\leq\tau(m)\nrm f_{\mathcal L}$ and $\tau(m_1m_2)\leq\tau(m_1)\tau(m_2)$; moreover $\tau(m)\leq\kappa_5\nrm m_{\mathrm{ch}}$ and $\nrm{m_1m_2}_{\mathrm{ch}}\leq\nrm{m_1}_{\mathrm{ch}}\nrm{m_2}_{\mathrm{ch}}$; and $\iota(mf)=m\,\iota(f)$.
\item For $c\in\mathcal E$ the bounds \eqref{r3:eq-chnorms} hold for the graded norms of this section.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(a) Up to the positive factors $C^{(3/2)}_v(1)/(C^{(3/2)}_a(1)C^{(3/2)}_\ell(1))$, the $G_{a\ell v}$ are the coefficients of the linearisation formula \cite[18.18.22]{DLMF} for $C^{(3/2)}_aC^{(3/2)}_\ell$, all of whose factors are positive. Evaluation at $\xi=1$ gives $\sum_vG_{a\ell v}=1$, and the last bound is proved as in Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-gaunt}, since all $D_{nv}$ are nonnegative.

(b) The proofs of Lemmas~\ref{r3:lem-mult}(a) and~\ref{r3:lem-radial} use only (a), the resulting identity $H_aH_\ell=\sum_vG_{a\ell v}H_vt^{(a+\ell-v)/2}$ and Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-jacobi}; in part~(b) of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-radial} the factors are now $\beta_v+1=v+\frac52\leq\beta_\ell+m+1$ and $(m-i)/(\beta_\ell+m+2+i)\leq d/(\ell+d+\frac72)$.

(c) Every $\Psi_{v,\varsigma}$ in the expansion of $H_at^k\Psi_{\ell,s}$ has degree $v+2\varsigma\leq(a+2k)+(\ell+2s)$, so $\Upsilon(v+2\varsigma)\leq\Upsilon(a+2k)\Upsilon(\ell+2s)$ and, by (a) and (b), $\nrm{H_at^k\Psi_{\ell,s}}_{\mathcal L}\leq h_a\Upsilon(a+2k)\,h_\ell\Upsilon(\ell+2s)$; absolute summation gives the first bound, and products of two solid monomials are treated in the same way. The expansion $r^{n+2k}T_n=\sum_iC_{n-2i,n}H_{n-2i}t^{i+k}$ preserves the degree $n+2k$, so Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-conn}(b) gives $\tau(r^{n+2k}T_n)\leq\kappa_5R^n\Upsilon(n+2k)$. For Chebyshev products, $T_mT_n=\frac12(T_{m+n}+T_{|m-n|})$ adds the degrees and $R^{|m-n|}\leq R^{m+n}$. The last identity is proved as Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-mult}(d).

(d) By \eqref{r3:eq-qseries}, the mode $w^j$ contributes to $q_c$, $\partial_{x_1}q_c$ and $y^{-1}\partial_yq_c$ homogeneous polynomials of degrees $j-1$, $j-2$ and $j-3$, and to $p_c$ the monomial $jw^{j-1}$. Their graded norms are at most $\Upsilon(j-1)$ times the ungraded ones, which is matched by the factor $\Upsilon(j-1)$ in $\nrm c_{\mathcal E}$, and the ungraded bounds are those of the proof of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-F}. For $|p_c|^2$ use \eqref{r3:eq-p2} and $R^{\alpha+\beta}\Upsilon(\alpha+\beta)\leq R^\alpha\Upsilon(\alpha)\,R^\beta\Upsilon(\beta)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{r5:lem-K}
For $\ell\geq0$, $s\geq1$ and $d$ as in \eqref{r5:eq-K}, with $\Psi_{-1,\cdot}=0$,
\begin{align*}
\partial_{x_1}K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}&=\frac{\lambda^+_\ell}{2(d+1)}\big(\Psi_{\ell+1,s}-\Psi_{\ell+1,s-1}\big)+\frac{\lambda^-_\ell}{2(d+1)}\big(\Psi_{\ell-1,s+1}-\Psi_{\ell-1,s}\big),\\
EK_5\Psi_{\ell,s}&=-\frac{d+\frac32}{4d(d+1)}\Psi_{\ell,s-1}+\frac{3\,\Psi_{\ell,s}}{4d(d+2)}+\frac{d+\frac12}{4(d+1)(d+2)}\Psi_{\ell,s+1},
\end{align*}
where $E=x\cdot\nabla$, and $y\partial_yK_5=EK_5-x_1\partial_{x_1}K_5$. The operators $K_5$, $\partial_{x_1}K_5$, $EK_5$ and $y\partial_yK_5$ are bounded from $\Gc$ to $\mathcal L$, with norms at most, for $d_0=\frac72$,
\begin{gather*}
\mathsf k_K=\frac1{d_0(d_0+2)}+\frac{\Upsilon(2)-1}{4(d_0+1)(d_0+2)},\qquad\mathsf k_D=\frac{R\,(1+\Upsilon(1))}{2(d_0+1)},\\
\mathsf k_E=\frac{d_0+\frac32}{2d_0(d_0+1)}+\frac{(\Upsilon(2)-1)(d_0+\frac12)}{4(d_0+1)(d_0+2)},\qquad\mathsf k_Y=\mathsf k_E+R\,\Upsilon(1)\,\mathsf k_D .
\end{gather*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By \eqref{r5:eq-Hrec} and $2\lambda^-_\ell\beta_\ell=\ell$, $\partial_{x_1}(H_\ell k)=2\lambda^+_\ell H_{\ell+1}k'+2\lambda^-_\ell H_{\ell-1}(\beta_\ell k+tk')$ and $E(H_\ell k)=H_\ell\big(2(\beta_\ell k+tk')-(\beta_\ell+\frac32)k\big)$. The expansions of $k_{\ell,s}'$ and of $\beta k_{\ell,s}+tk_{\ell,s}'$ in the proof of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-K}(a),(d) hold for every $\beta$ and give the two formulas. For the norms we use Lemma~\ref{lem:columns}. The stencil of $K_5$ changes the degree $D=\ell+2s$ by $-2$, $0$, $2$, has column sum zero and absolute column sum $1/(d(d+2))$; with $\Upsilon(D-2)\leq\Upsilon(D)$ and $\Upsilon(D+2)\leq\Upsilon(2)\Upsilon(D)$ its weighted column sum is at most $1/(d(d+2))+(\Upsilon(2)-1)/(4(d+1)(d+2))$, which decreases in $d\geq d_0$. The stencil of $EK_5$ also has column sum zero, with absolute column sum $(d+\frac32)/(2d(d+1))$, which gives $\mathsf k_E$ in the same way. For $\partial_{x_1}K_5$ the degrees change by $\pm1$, $\Upsilon(D+1)+\Upsilon(D-1)\leq(1+\Upsilon(1))\Upsilon(D)$, and $\lambda^+_\ell h_{\ell+1}+\lambda^-_\ell h_{\ell-1}\leq Rh_\ell$ by Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-conn}(c). Finally $\tau(x_1)=h_1\Upsilon(1)=R\,\Upsilon(1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Transfer to graded norms]\label{r5:thm-transfer}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item Let $M$ be a linear map between weighted $\ell^1$ spaces whose weights are base weights times $\Upsilon$ of the degree of the basis elements, and suppose that $M_{\kappa\iota}\neq0$ implies $\deg\kappa\leq\deg\iota+\Delta$. Then $\nrm M\leq\Upsilon(\max(\Delta,0))\nrm M_0$, where $\nrm\cdot_0$ is the operator norm for the base weights. The same holds for multilinear maps, with $\deg\iota$ replaced by the sum of the degrees of the inputs.
\item Let $(s_\delta)_{\delta\in\Z}$ be finitely supported with $s_0>0$, and let
\[
\eps_\Upsilon=\frac1{s_0}\sum_{\delta\neq0}|s_\delta|R^{2\delta}\,\Upsilon\big(\max(2\delta,0)\big)<1 .
\]
For $N_0\geq0$ let $E$ act on sequences $(\hat a_N)_{N\geq N_0,\ N\ \mathrm{even}}$ by
\[
(E\hat a)_m=\frac1{s_0}\sum_{N\neq m}s_{(m-N)/2}R^{m-N}\hat a_N\qquad(m\geq N_0\ \text{even}).
\]
On the $\ell^1$ space with weights $\Upsilon(N)$, $\nrm E\leq\eps_\Upsilon$; hence $I+E$ is invertible, $\nrm{(I+E)^{-1}}\leq(1-\eps_\Upsilon)^{-1}$, and the remainder of the Neumann series after the terms $k\leq L$ has norm at most $\eps_\Upsilon^{L+1}/(1-\eps_\Upsilon)$.
\item In particular, the outputs of a product of a solid monomial of degree $d$ with $\Psi_{\ell,s}$ carry at most the factor $\Upsilon(d)$ relative to the weight of $\Psi_{\ell,s}$, and a polynomial output of degree at most $N+\Delta$ produced from a shape input of degree $N$ carries at most the factor $\Upsilon(\Delta)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(a) By Lemma~\ref{lem:columns} it suffices to consider single columns (tuples of basis vectors for multilinear maps). The weighted column norm is a sum of base-weighted entries multiplied by $\Upsilon(\deg\kappa)/\Upsilon(\deg\iota)$, which is at most $\Upsilon(\deg\kappa-\deg\iota)\leq\Upsilon(\Delta)$ if $\deg\kappa\geq\deg\iota$, by submultiplicativity, and at most one otherwise. (b) Column $N$ of $E$ has entries only in the rows $m=N+2\delta\geq N_0$, and $\Upsilon(N+2\delta)/\Upsilon(N)\leq\Upsilon(\max(2\delta,0))$; so its weighted column sum is at most $\eps_\Upsilon\Upsilon(N)$. The rest is the Neumann series. (c) is (a) applied to the degree bound of Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-mult}(c).
\end{proof}

%----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{The quartic equation and the approximate inverse}\label{r5:sec-quartic}
%----------------------------------------------------------------------

From now on $M=160$, $S=57$ and $\rho=7951044098899425\cdot2^{-47}\approx56.4955663$. The exact dyadic centre $x^\circ=(g^\circ,c^\circ)$ consists of field coefficients $g^\circ_{\ell,s}$ with even $\ell\leq M$ and $1\leq s\leq S$ and odd shape coefficients $c^\circ_j$ with $j\leq M+1$; it is embedded in the verifier of Section~\ref{r5:sec-cap} (SHA-256 recorded there). We put
\[
b=c^\circ_1\approx1.0002252,\qquad\sigma=30\rho^2,
\]
a fixed dyadic number and a fixed scale, independent of the varying coefficient $c_1$. For $x=(g,c)\in\mathcal X$ let $W=K_5g$, $W_1=\partial_{x_1}K_5g$ and $W_2=y\partial_yK_5g$, elements of $\mathcal L$ by Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-K}, and
\begin{equation}\label{r5:eq-F}
\Fc(g,c)=\frac1b\Big[q_c\,g+3(\partial_{x_1}q_c)\,W_1+3\Big(\frac{\partial_yq_c}y\Big)W_2+\rho^2q_c|p_c|^2(1+W)\Big].
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}\label{r5:lem-F}
$\Fc$ is a bounded polynomial map of degree four from $\mathcal X$ to $\mathcal Y$. If $\Fc(g,c)=0$ and $\re\psi_c'>0$ on $\{|w|<R_0\}$ for some $1<R_0<R$, then $U=1+\iota(K_5g)\in C^1(\overline B)$ is axisymmetric, satisfies \eqref{r5:eq-weak} for all $\varphi\in C_c^\infty(B)$, and $U=1$, $\nabla U=0$ on $\partial B$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Boundedness follows from Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-mult}(c),(d) and Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-K}, and the values are even in $x_1$ as in Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-F}. Suppose $\Fc(g,c)=0$. By Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-C1}, $W=\iota(K_5g)\in C^1(\overline B)$ has vanishing Cauchy data and $\Delta W=\iota(g)$. Let $\varphi\in C_c^\infty(B)$; then $q_c^3\varphi\in C_c^\infty(B)$ by Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-lift}(a), and, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-F},
\begin{align*}
\int_Bq_c^3\big(\nabla U\cdot\nabla\varphi-\rho^2|p_c|^2U\varphi\big)&=\int_B\big(\nabla W\cdot\nabla(q_c^3\varphi)-3q_c^2\varphi\,\nabla q_c\cdot\nabla W-\rho^2q_c^3|p_c|^2U\varphi\big)\\
&=-\int_Bq_c^2\varphi\,b\,\iota(\Fc(g,c))=0,
\end{align*}
using $\int\nabla W\cdot\nabla(q_c^3\varphi)=-\int\iota(g)q_c^3\varphi$ and the identity for $\nabla q_c\cdot\nabla W$ before \eqref{r5:eq-div}. No averaging over $O(4)$ is needed: the test function $\varphi$ is arbitrary.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{Nonlinear bounds}
Let $G_0\geq\nrm{g^\circ}_{\mathcal L}$ and $C_0\geq\nrm{c^\circ}_{\mathcal E}$, let $\mathsf k_K,\mathsf k_D,\mathsf k_Y$ be as in Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-K}, and put
\begin{gather*}
B'=\frac1R+3\Big(\frac{2\mathsf k_D}{R^2-1}+\frac{4R\,\mathsf k_Y}{(R^2-1)^2}\Big),\qquad c_2=\frac{\kappa_5}b\Big[\frac{B'}\sigma+\frac{3\rho^2\mathsf k_KC_0^2}{R^3\sigma}+\frac{3\rho^2C_0(1+\mathsf k_KG_0)}{R^3\sigma^2}\Big],\\
c_3=\frac{\kappa_5\rho^2}{bR^3}\Big[\frac{1+\mathsf k_KG_0}{\sigma^3}+\frac{3\mathsf k_KC_0}{\sigma^2}\Big],\qquad c_4=\frac{\kappa_5\rho^2\mathsf k_K}{bR^3\sigma^3}.
\end{gather*}
Then the two estimates of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-nonlinear} hold for \eqref{r5:eq-F}. The proof is that of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-nonlinear}, with $L_2=\kappa_5B'$, $L_3=\kappa_5\rho^2R^{-3}$ and $L_4=\mathsf k_K\kappa_5\rho^2R^{-3}$ (Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-mult} and Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-K}; each multiplier is converted from Chebyshev to solid form once). Proposition~\ref{r3:prop-NK} applies verbatim to $\Fc\colon\mathcal X\to\mathcal Y$.

\subsubsection*{Finite and tail coordinates}
The \emph{finite inputs} are the $4617$ field coordinates $(\ell,s)$ with $\ell\leq M$ even and $1\leq s\leq S$, and the $81$ shape coordinates $c_j$ with $j\leq M+1$; the \emph{finite rows} are the same $4617$ field rows and the $81$ harmonic rows $\mathcal T_n$, $n\leq M$ even. All other inputs and rows are tail inputs and rows, and tail rows are harmonic or nonharmonic, as in Section~\ref{r3:sec-inverse}. On the shape tail and on the harmonic rows we use the coordinates $\hat a_N=(N+1)R^{N+1}c_{N+1}$ and $\hat y_n=R^ny_n$ ($N$, $n$ even), in which the corresponding parts of the norms are $\sigma\sum_N\Upsilon(N)|\hat a_N|$ and $\sum_n\Upsilon(n)|\hat y_n|$.

\subsubsection*{The principal part on high shape modes}
Let $q^\circ=q_{c^\circ}$ and $p^\circ=p_{c^\circ}=\sum_ap_aw^a$, $p_a=(a+1)c^\circ_{a+1}$. For $j=N+1$ the derivative of $b^{-1}\rho^2q_c|p_c|^2$ at $c^\circ$ in the direction $e_j$ contains the principal polynomial $\Pi_j=\frac{2(N+1)\rho^2}b\,q^\circ\re(\overline{p^\circ}w^N)$. On $\partial B$, $q^\circ=\sum_kq_ke^{ik\theta}$ with $q_k=\sum_{i>|k|}c^\circ_i$ ($k$ even, $i$ odd), and
\[
\Pi_j|_{\partial B}=\frac{2(N+1)\rho^2}b\sum_\delta s_\delta T_{|N+2\delta|}(\xi),\qquad s_\delta=\sum_{k-a=2\delta}p_aq_k\quad(-160\leq\delta\leq80).
\]
Let $\mathsf Me_j\in\mathcal Y$ be the harmonic element with these boundary values, and define $\eps_\Upsilon$ from $(s_\delta)$ as in Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-transfer}(b). As in Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-principal}, for $N\geq M+2$ the part of $\mathsf M$ from the shape tail to the harmonic tail rows is, in the coordinates $\hat a$, $\hat y$,
\[
T=\frac{2\rho^2s_0}{bR}\,(I+E),
\]
with $E$ as in Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-transfer}(b) for $N_0=M+2$, and the part $\mathcal B$ from the shape tail to the finite harmonic rows collects the rows $|N+2\delta|\leq M$. If $\eps_\Upsilon<1$, then $T$ is invertible on the graded tail, with $\nrm{T^{-1}}\leq\tau_T:=bR/(2\rho^2s_0(1-\eps_\Upsilon))$, and $T^{-1}$ is evaluated by the Neumann series with the remainder bound of Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-transfer}(b).

\subsubsection*{The operator $A$}
Let $\widetilde J$ be the finite Jacobian $D\Fc(x^\circ)$, restricted to finite inputs and rows, in scaled coordinates (inputs multiplied by their $\mathcal X$-weights and rows by their $\mathcal Y$-weights, so that both norms become plain $\ell^1$ norms). It is computed at the exact centre with interval entries. Let $\widehat A$ be a binary64 approximation of the inverse of its midpoint matrix, regarded as an exact dyadic matrix, and define, for $y=y_f+y^H_t+y^N_t\in\mathcal Y$,
\begin{equation}\label{r5:eq-A}
Ay=\widehat A\big(y_f-\mathcal BT^{-1}y^H_t\big)+T^{-1}y^H_t+y^N_t,
\end{equation}
as in \eqref{r3:eq-A}. For a finite row $\iota$ let $\alpha_\iota$ be an upper bound for the normalised norm of $\widehat Ae_\iota$, let $\alpha^H_n=\alpha_{\mathcal T_n}$ for $n\leq M$, and put
\[
\rho_{\mathcal B}=\sup_{N>M}\frac1{\Upsilon(N)}\sum_{n\leq M}\alpha^H_n\Upsilon(n)|\hat{\mathcal B}_{nN}|,\qquad A_t=\tau_T(\sigma+\rho_{\mathcal B}),
\]
where $\hat{\mathcal B}$ is the matrix of $\mathcal B$ in the coordinates $\hat a$, $\hat y$; we set $\alpha^H_n=A_t$ for $n>M$.

\begin{lemma}\label{r5:lem-A}
Suppose that $\eps_\Upsilon<1$ and that the column sums of $|I-\widehat A\widetilde J|$ are less than one. Then $A\colon\mathcal Y\to\mathcal X$ is a bounded bijection with $\nrm A\leq\max\{\max_\iota\alpha_\iota,A_t,1\}$. It maps every normalised harmonic tail row to a vector of norm at most $A_t$ and every nonharmonic tail row to the identical field tail input; $A\mathsf Me_j=e_j$ for every odd $j\geq M+3$, and $A\Psi_{\ell,s}=e_{(\ell,s)}$ for every field tail input $(\ell,s)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
As for Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-A}. For a normalised harmonic tail row $\hat y=e_m$, the shape part $T^{-1}e_m$ has norm at most $\sigma\tau_T\Upsilon(m)$, and $\widehat A\mathcal BT^{-1}e_m$ has norm at most $\rho_{\mathcal B}\tau_T\Upsilon(m)$.
\end{proof}

%----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{The defect bounds}\label{r5:sec-Z}
%----------------------------------------------------------------------

We bound $Y=\nrm{A\Fc(x^\circ)}_{\mathcal X}$ and $Z=\nrm{I-AD\Fc(x^\circ)}_{\mathcal X\to\mathcal X}$ by normalised column norms (Lemma~\ref{lem:columns}). The input columns are partitioned into disjoint classes that exhaust all inputs; the class parameters are frozen in the file \texttt{params.json} and rechecked by the verifier.
\begin{itemize}
\item Field inputs: the $4617$ finite inputs; for even $\ell\leq160$, the radial class $s\geq S_\ell$, with $S_\ell=59$ for $\ell\leq10$ and $S_\ell=58$ for $12\leq\ell\leq160$, and the six exceptional inputs $(\ell,58)$, $\ell\leq10$; for even $162\leq\ell\leq254$, the angular gap class $s\geq S_\ell$, with $S_\ell=3$ for $\ell\leq178$, $S_\ell=2$ for $180\leq\ell\leq248$ and $S_\ell=1$ for $250\leq\ell\leq254$, and the $53$ exceptional inputs $(\ell,s)$ with $s<S_\ell$; and the uniform angular class $\ell\geq256$, $s\geq1$.
\item Shape inputs: the $81$ finite inputs $j\leq161$, the $219$ explicit inputs $163\leq j\leq599$, and the far class $j\geq601$.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection*{The residual and the explicit columns}
The residual $\Fc(x^\circ)$, which has $70299$ nonzero coefficients, and the columns $D\Fc(x^\circ)e$ of all $4698$ finite inputs, of the $59$ exceptional field inputs and of the $219$ explicit shape inputs are polynomials. They are expanded with their complete support in ball arithmetic at the final centre. For the field columns, the products of the four multipliers $q^\circ$, $\partial_{x_1}q^\circ$, $y^{-1}\partial_yq^\circ$, $q^\circ|p^\circ|^2$ with $\Psi_{\ell,s}$ are obtained for all $s$ from the exact three-term expression of $t\Psi_{\ell,s}$ in $\Psi_{\ell,s-1},\Psi_{\ell,s},\Psi_{\ell,s+1}$ (Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-jacobi}(b)), at $1024$ bits; the shape columns are obtained from exact recurrences in $m$ for $r^mU_m$, $r^mC^{(2)}_m$ and $\re(\overline{p^\circ}w^m)$, at $1536$ bits. Every column is recorded by enclosures of its finite rows and of all its harmonic tail coordinates, together with an upper bound for the norm of its nonharmonic tail part; a relative-width test rejects any loss of precision. Let $y=y_f+y^H_t+y^N_t$ be such a column and $w_e$ the weight of the input $e$. By \eqref{r5:eq-A}:
\begin{itemize}
\item for a finite input, $\nrm{(I-AD\Fc(x^\circ))e}_{\mathcal X}/w_e\leq\delta_e+(A_t\nrm{y^H_t}_{\mathcal Y}+\nrm{y^N_t}_{\mathcal Y})/w_e$, where $\delta_e$ bounds the column sum of $|I-\widehat A\widetilde J|$ and is certified from the interval columns by a long-double matrix product with rigorous rounding-error bounds (unit roundoff $2^{-64}$);
\item for an exceptional field input, whose image under $A$ is the identical field tail input, the bound is $(\nrm{\widehat Ay_f}+A_t\nrm{y^H_t}+\nrm{y^N_t-e})/w_e$;
\item for an explicit shape input $e_j$, with $a$ the Neumann approximation of $T^{-1}y^H_t$ and $\mathrm{rem}$ its remainder bound, the bound is $\big(\nrm{\widehat A(y_f-\mathcal Ba)}+\sigma\nrm{a-e_j}+\nrm{y^N_t}+(\sigma+\rho_{\mathcal B})\mathrm{rem}\big)/w_j$; the same expression without $e_j$ and $w_j$, applied to $y=\Fc(x^\circ)$, bounds $Y$.
\end{itemize}
We denote the maxima over the three groups of columns by $Z_{\mathrm{fin}}$, $Z_{\mathrm{ex}}$ and $Z_{\mathrm{sh}}$.

\subsubsection*{Field tail classes}
For a field tail input, $A\Psi_{\ell,s}=e_{(\ell,s)}$, so that $(I-AD\Fc(x^\circ))e_{(\ell,s)}=-Av$ with
\[
v=\chi_1\Psi_{\ell,s}+3\chi_2\,\partial_{x_1}K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}+3\chi_3\,EK_5\Psi_{\ell,s}-3\chi_4\,\partial_{x_1}K_5\Psi_{\ell,s}+\rho^2\chi_5\,K_5\Psi_{\ell,s},
\]
where $\chi_1=q^\circ/b-1$, $\chi_2=\partial_{x_1}q^\circ/b$, $\chi_3=y^{-1}\partial_yq^\circ/b$, $\chi_4=x_1\chi_3$ and $\chi_5=q^\circ|p^\circ|^2/b$. For $1\leq i\leq5$ let $\tau_i(d)$ be the graded $\tau$-norm of the homogeneous part of degree $d$ of $\chi_i$, multiplied by $3$ for $i=2,3,4$ and by $\rho^2$ for $i=5$; it is computed exactly from the solid-monomial expansion. Define $\mathsf A(\ell,\varsigma)$, $\mathsf B(L)$ and $\mathfrak B_d(\ell,s)$ as before Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-envelope}, now with the column norms $\alpha_\iota$ of this section, with $\vartheta_d$ as in Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-mult}(b), and with
\begin{gather*}
\beta_v=\frac1{h_v\Upsilon(v)}\sum_nD_{nv}R^n\Upsilon(n)\alpha^H_n\quad(v\leq1400),\\
\beta^\infty=A_t+\sum_{n\leq M}(\alpha^H_n-A_t)_+\,\mathsf a_{k_n}\zeta^{k_n},\qquad k_n=\frac{1402-n}2,
\end{gather*}
in place of $\beta_\ell$ and $\beta^\infty$ (and $1400$ in place of $1200$ in the definition of $\mathsf B$). Let $\mathfrak B^{(i)}(\ell,s)=\sum_d\tau_i(d)\mathfrak B_d(\ell,s)$, $\bar d=\ell+2s+\frac32$, and
\begin{align*}
\mathfrak z(\ell,s)={}&\mathfrak B^{(1)}(\ell,s)+\frac{\Upsilon(1)}{2(\bar d+1)}\sum_{i=2,4}\Big[\lambda^+_\ell\frac{h_{\ell+1}}{h_\ell}\big(\mathfrak B^{(i)}(\ell+1,s)+\mathfrak B^{(i)}(\ell+1,s-1)\big)\\
&\hphantom{\mathfrak B^{(1)}(\ell,s)+\frac{\Upsilon(1)}{2(\bar d+1)}\sum_{i=2,4}\Big[}+\lambda^-_\ell\frac{h_{\ell-1}}{h_\ell}\big(\mathfrak B^{(i)}(\ell-1,s+1)+\mathfrak B^{(i)}(\ell-1,s)\big)\Big]\\
&+\frac{(\bar d+\frac32)\mathfrak B^{(3)}(\ell,s-1)}{4\bar d(\bar d+1)}+\frac{3\,\mathfrak B^{(3)}(\ell,s)}{4\bar d(\bar d+2)}+\frac{\Upsilon(2)(\bar d+\frac12)\mathfrak B^{(3)}(\ell,s+1)}{4(\bar d+1)(\bar d+2)}\\
&+\frac{\mathfrak B^{(5)}(\ell,s-1)}{4\bar d(\bar d+1)}+\frac{\mathfrak B^{(5)}(\ell,s)}{2\bar d(\bar d+2)}+\frac{\Upsilon(2)\,\mathfrak B^{(5)}(\ell,s+1)}{4(\bar d+1)(\bar d+2)},
\end{align*}
where terms with $\ell-1<0$ are omitted. Let $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm u}(\ell,s)$ be the same expression with the bracket replaced by $R\max\{\mathfrak B^{(i)}(\ell+1,s)+\mathfrak B^{(i)}(\ell+1,s-1),\ \mathfrak B^{(i)}(\ell-1,s+1)+\mathfrak B^{(i)}(\ell-1,s)\}$.

\begin{proposition}\label{r5:prop-gtail}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item For every field tail input, $\nrm{(I-AD\Fc(x^\circ))e_{(\ell,s)}}_{\mathcal X}/(h_\ell\Upsilon(\ell+2s))\leq\mathfrak z(\ell,s)\leq\mathfrak z_{\mathrm u}(\ell,s)$.
\item For fixed $\ell$, $\mathfrak z(\ell,s)$ is nonincreasing in $s$, and $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm u}(\ell,s)$ is nonincreasing in $\ell$ and in $s$. Hence the radial class and the angular gap class of $\ell$ are bounded by $\mathfrak z(\ell,S_\ell)$, and the uniform angular class by $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm u}(256,1)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) As in Proposition~\ref{r3:prop-gtail}(a): expand $v$ with Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-K} and bound each product of a homogeneous part of $\chi_i$ with a $\Psi$ by Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-envelope}, which holds in the graded norms with $h_ah_\ell$ replaced by $h_a\Upsilon(a+2k)\,h_\ell\Upsilon(\ell+2s)$, by Theorems~\ref{r5:thm-mult}(b) and~\ref{r5:thm-transfer}(c). In its proof, Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-rows} is used; the bound for $v>1400$ now follows from $\sum_nD_{nv}R^n\Upsilon(n)\leq h_v\Upsilon(v)$, Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-conn}(c) and the monotonicity of $\mathsf a_k\zeta^k$ for $k\geq4$. The factors $\Upsilon(1)$ and $\Upsilon(2)$ bound the ratios of the weights of the outputs of the stencils to $\Upsilon(\ell+2s)$. The second inequality follows from $\lambda^+_\ell h_{\ell+1}+\lambda^-_\ell h_{\ell-1}\leq Rh_\ell$ (Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-conn}(c)).

(b) Each $\mathfrak B_d$ is nonincreasing in both arguments: $\mathsf A$, $\mathsf B$ and $\vartheta_d$ are nonincreasing (the step functions are suffix maxima), the first bound in $\mathfrak B_d$ is nondecreasing in $\mathsf A$, $\mathsf B$ and $\vartheta_d$, and the second is an expectation of a function that is nonincreasing in both arguments. The stencil coefficients decrease in $\bar d$, and in $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm u}$ no other dependence on $\ell$ remains.
\end{proof}

We write $\max_\ell Z_{\mathrm{rad}}(\ell)$ and $\max_\ell Z_{\mathrm{gap}}(\ell)$ for the maxima of $\mathfrak z(\ell,S_\ell)$ over the radial and angular gap classes, and $Z_{\mathrm{ang}}=\mathfrak z_{\mathrm u}(256,1)$.

\subsubsection*{Far shape columns}
Let $j=N+1$ with $N\geq N_*=600$ even. Let $W^\circ=K_5g^\circ$, $W^\circ_1=\partial_{x_1}W^\circ$, $Z^\circ=y^{-1}\partial_yW^\circ$ (a polynomial, since $W^\circ$ is a polynomial in $x_1$ and $y^2$; $y\partial_yW^\circ=t(1-\xi^2)Z^\circ$), and $H^\circ=g^\circ+\rho^2|p^\circ|^2(1+W^\circ)$. With $\delta q=r^NU_N$, $\delta\partial_{x_1}q=(N+1)r^{N-1}U_{N-1}$, $\delta(y^{-1}\partial_yq)=-2r^{N-2}C^{(2)}_{N-2}$ and $\delta p=(N+1)w^N$, Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-axis}(b) gives
\begin{equation}\label{r5:eq-farcol}
\begin{split}
b\,D\Fc(x^\circ)e_j={}&r^NU_N\,H^\circ+3(N+1)r^{N-1}U_{N-1}\,W^\circ_1+3\Big(Nr^NT_N-\frac{r^NU_N+t\,r^{N-2}U_{N-2}}2\Big)Z^\circ\\
&+2(N+1)\rho^2q^\circ\re(\overline{p^\circ}w^N)\,W^\circ+b\,\Pi_j .
\end{split}
\end{equation}
Since $A\mathsf Me_j=e_j$, $(I-AD\Fc(x^\circ))e_j=-A(D\Fc(x^\circ)e_j-\mathsf Me_j)$. From $\re(\bw^aw^N)=r^{N+a}T_{N-a}(\xi)$, $r^{i-1}U_{i-1}=\sum_k(2-\delta_{2k,i-1})t^kr^{i-1-2k}T_{i-1-2k}$ and $T_KT_n=\frac12(T_{K+n}+T_{K-n})$ we obtain
\[
q^\circ\re(\overline{p^\circ}w^N)=\sum_{(\eta,e)}\gamma_{\eta,e}\,t^er^{N+\eta}T_{N+\eta}(\xi),
\]
a finite sum over $-320\leq\eta\leq160$ and $e\geq0$ with coefficients independent of $N$; let $\gamma_{\eta,e}\geq0$ be the sum of the moduli of the individual contributions to the coefficient of $t^er^{N+\eta}T_{N+\eta}$. The element $\mathsf Me_j$ is obtained from $\Pi_j$ by replacing each $t^er^KT_K$ with $\mathcal T_K$.

\emph{Column profiles of $A$.} For $v\geq0$ let $\Gamma_F(v)\geq1$ and $\Gamma_H(v)\geq A_t$ be nonincreasing bounds, computed from the $\alpha_\iota$, for the normalised norms $\nrm{A\Psi_{v',\varsigma}}_{\mathcal X}/(h_{v'}\Upsilon(v'+2\varsigma))$ ($\varsigma\geq1$) and for $\beta_{v'}$, over all $v'\geq v$; let $\Gamma_*=\max(\Gamma_F,\Gamma_H)$, $\Gamma_{\max}=\Gamma_*(0)$, $\alpha^H(K)=\alpha^H_K$, and let $\mathcal H_v=\sum_nD_{nv}\mathcal T_n$ be the harmonic element with boundary values $Y_v$, so that $\nrm{A\mathcal H_v}\leq\Gamma_H(v)h_v\Upsilon(v)$. Let $I=140$, $S_p(n)=\sum_{k\geq0}\zeta^k(n+k)^p$ and, for $p\in\{0,1,2\}$ and $i_1=\lfloor(K-M)/2\rfloor+1$,
\[
G_p(K)=\begin{cases}\mathsf w_{I+1}S_p(I+1)\,\Gamma_{\max},&i_1\leq I+1,\\ \mathsf w_{I+1}\big(S_p(I+1)\,\Gamma_*(M+1)+\zeta^{i_1-I-1}S_p(i_1)\,\Gamma_{\max}\big),&i_1>I+1.\end{cases}
\]
Since $e_i$ is nonincreasing for $i\geq1$, $\mathsf w_i\leq\mathsf w_{I+1}\zeta^{i-I-1}$ for $i>I$, and $\sum_{I<i\leq K/2}\mathsf w_ii^p\Gamma_*(K-2i)\leq G_p(K)$, because $K-2i\geq M$ for $i<i_1$ and $\Gamma_*(M)=\Gamma_*(M+1)$ for the certified profiles.

\begin{lemma}\label{r5:lem-lap}
For $K\geq2$, $\sum_ii\,(K-i+\frac32)\,C_{K-2i,K}\,Y_{K-2i}=-\frac{3K}4U_{K-2}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In $\R^5$, $\Delta(t^iH_v)=4i(i+v+\frac32)t^{i-1}H_v$. The function $r^KT_K(\xi)=\re w^K$ is harmonic in the meridian variables, so $\Delta\re w^K=\frac3y\partial_y\re w^K=-\frac{3K}y\im w^{K-1}=-3Kr^{K-2}U_{K-2}(\xi)$. Apply $\Delta$ to $r^KT_K=\sum_iC_{K-2i,K}H_{K-2i}t^i$ and restrict to $t=1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{r5:lem-raw}
Let $K\geq0$, $e\geq0$, $m\geq0$, $\beta=K+\frac32$, $c_m=\max(2,m+1)$, $\mathsf F_m(\beta')=m!/(\beta'/2+1)_m$, and
\[
\mathsf f(x)=\frac{\beta-2x+1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m+1}(\beta-x+e+k)},\qquad\varrho_2(i)=\mathsf f(i)-\mathsf f(0)-\mathsf f'(0)\,i .
\]
Let $u=t^er^KT_K(\xi)(1-t)^m$ if $m\geq1$, and $u=t^er^KT_K(\xi)-\mathcal T_K$ if $m=0$ and $K>M$. Then
\begin{multline*}
\frac{\nrm{Au}_{\mathcal X}}{R^K\Upsilon(K+2e+2m)}\leq\sum_i\mathsf w_i\,\mathsf g_i\,\Gamma_F(K-2i)\\
+m!\Big(\mathsf f_0\,\alpha^H(K)+\frac34|\mathsf f'(0)|\,\mathsf u(K)+\sum_{i\geq1}\mathsf w_i\Big(\frac{|\mathsf f'(0)|\,i^2}\beta+|\varrho_2(i)|\Big)\Gamma_H(K-2i)\Big),
\end{multline*}
where $\mathsf g_i=\mathsf F_m(\beta-2i)$ and $\mathsf f_0=\mathsf f(0)$ if $m\geq1$, $\mathsf g_i=(e+i)/(\beta-i+e+1)$ and $\mathsf f_0=1-\mathsf f(0)=e/(\beta+e+1)$ if $m=0$, and $\mathsf u(K)=2A_t\zeta/(1-\zeta)+2\sum_{n\leq\min(M,K-2),\,n\equiv K}R^{n-K}(\alpha^H_n-A_t)_+$. Moreover, for $i\leq K/2$,
\[
|\mathsf f'(0)|\leq\frac{c_m\mathsf f(0)}{\beta+1},\qquad|\varrho_2(i)|\leq\frac{i^2}2\cdot\frac{(\beta+1)(c_m^2+m+5)}{(\beta-2i+1)^2\prod_{k=1}^{m+1}(\beta-i+e+k)} .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $v_i=K-2i$. By definition of $C_{\ell K}$, $u=\sum_iC_{v_i,K}H_{v_i}t^{e+i}(1-t)^m$, minus $\mathcal T_K=\sum_iC_{v_i,K}H_{v_i}$ if $m=0$, and all outputs have degree at most $K+2e+2m$; so it suffices to bound the norms with $\Upsilon\equiv1$ (Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-transfer}(a)). \emph{Field layer.} By Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-jacobi}(c),(d), the $J^{\beta_{v_i}}$-coefficients of $t^{e+i}(1-t)^m$ have $\ell^1$ norm at most $\mathsf F_m(\beta_{v_i})=\mathsf F_m(\beta-2i)$, and those of $t^{e+i}-1$ with $s\geq1$ have sum $(e+i)/(\beta_{v_i}+e+i+1)$; with $|C_{v_i,K}|h_{v_i}\leq\mathsf w_iR^K$ this gives the first sum. \emph{Harmonic layer.} By Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-jacobi}(a), the coefficient of $J^{\beta_v}_0$ in $t^\mu(1-t)^m$ is $(\beta_v+1)\int_0^1t^{\beta_v+\mu}(1-t)^mdt=m!(\beta_v+1)/\prod_{k=1}^{m+1}(\beta_v+\mu+k)$, which for $v=v_i$ and $\mu=e+i$ equals $m!\,\mathsf f(i)$. Hence the boundary trace of the harmonic layer of $u$ is $m!\sum_iC_{v_i,K}\mathsf f(i)Y_{v_i}$, minus $T_K$ if $m=0$. By $\sum_iC_{v_i,K}Y_{v_i}=T_K$ and Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-lap}, $\sum_iiC_{v_i,K}Y_{v_i}=\beta^{-1}\big(-\frac{3K}4U_{K-2}+\sum_ii^2C_{v_i,K}Y_{v_i}\big)$, and the trace equals
\[
m!\Big(\mathsf f(0)T_K-\frac{3K\mathsf f'(0)}{4\beta}U_{K-2}+\sum_iC_{v_i,K}\Big(\frac{\mathsf f'(0)i^2}\beta+\varrho_2(i)\Big)Y_{v_i}\Big),
\]
with $\mathsf f(0)-1$ in place of $\mathsf f(0)$ if $m=0$. We apply $A$ to the three parts: $\nrm{A\mathcal T_K}\leq\alpha^H(K)R^K\Upsilon(K)$; the Chebyshev coefficients of $U_{K-2}$ are $2$ (and $1$ for $T_0$), which gives $\mathsf u(K)$, since $K<\beta$; and $Y_{v}$ is the trace of $\mathcal H_v$. (Thus the harmonic layer is, up to terms of relative size $O(i^2/\beta)$, a single Chebyshev mode; the triangle inequality applied directly to $\sum_iC_{v_i,K}\mathsf f(i)Y_{v_i}$ would lose a factor close to $\kappa_5$.) For the bounds on $\mathsf f$ write $\mathsf f=g/P$ with $g(x)=\beta-2x+1$ and $P(x)=\prod_k(\beta-x+e+k)$, and let $S_1=\sum_k(\beta-x+e+k)^{-1}$, $S_2=\sum_k(\beta-x+e+k)^{-2}$. Then $\mathsf f'=\mathsf f\,(S_1-2/g)$ and $\mathsf f''=P^{-1}\big(g(S_1^2+S_2)-4S_1\big)$. At $x=0$, $0<S_1\leq(m+1)/(\beta+1)$ and $2/g=2/(\beta+1)$, which gives the bound for $\mathsf f'(0)$. For $0\leq x\leq i\leq K/2$ the two terms of $\mathsf f''$ have opposite signs, so $|\mathsf f''(x)|\leq P(x)^{-1}\max\{g(S_1^2+S_2),4S_1\}$; with $P(x)\geq P(i)$, $g\leq\beta+1$, $S_1\leq(m+1)/(\beta-i+1)$, $S_2\leq(m+1)/(\beta-i+1)^2$ and $(\beta-2i+1)^2\leq(\beta+1)(\beta-i+1)$ this is at most $(\beta+1)\max\{(m+1)(m+2),4(m+1)\}/((\beta-2i+1)^2P(i))$, and $\max\{(m+1)(m+2),4(m+1)\}\leq c_m^2+m+5$. Taylor's formula gives the bound for $\varrho_2$.
\end{proof}

Let $\Phi_m(K)$ ($m\geq1$) and $\Phi_P(K,e)$ ($m=0$) be the right-hand side of Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-raw}, evaluated as follows. For $m\geq1$ the bounds are made independent of $e$ by putting $e=0$ in $\mathsf f(0)$ and in the bound for $\varrho_2$. The sums over $i$ are evaluated exactly for $i\leq24$; for $24<i\leq\min(I,K/2)$ the factors $\mathsf g_i$ and $|\varrho_2(i)|/i^2$, which increase with $i$, are replaced by their values at the largest index; and the terms with $i>I$ are bounded by means of $G_0,G_1,G_2$, using $\mathsf g_i\leq1$, $|\mathsf f(i)|\leq\mathsf f_{\max}$ and $|\varrho_2(i)|\leq2\mathsf f_{\max}+|\mathsf f'(0)|i$, where $\mathsf f_{\max}=(\beta+1)/(K/2+\frac52)^{m+1}$ for $m\geq1$ and $\mathsf f_{\max}=1$ for $m=0$. Let $\varphi_m(K)=\sup_{K'\geq K}\Phi_m(K')$, and
\[
\bar\Gamma(K)=\sup_{K'\geq K}\sum_{i\leq I}\mathsf w_i\Gamma_*\big(\max(K'-2i,0)\big)+\mathsf w_{I+1}S_0(I+1)\,\Gamma_{\max} .
\]
For $f$ one of the polynomials $H^\circ$, $W^\circ_1$, $Z^\circ$, $W^\circ$, write $f=\sum_aH_af_a(t)$, $f_a=\sum_sf_{a,s}J^{\beta_a}_s=\sum_mf^\natural_{a,m}(1-t)^m$ and $\nrm{f_a}_J=\sum_s|f_{a,s}|$. By Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-C1}, every $f_a$ has a double zero at $t=1$ for $f=W^\circ$ and a zero for $f=W^\circ_1,Z^\circ$, so the corresponding $f^\natural_{a,m}$ vanish exactly. For $K'\geq0$ let
\[
\mathrm{HH}_a(K')=\sup_{K''\geq K'}\sum_{i\leq\min(100,K''/2)}\mathsf w_i\Gamma_*(K''-2i)\sum_{m>3}|f^\natural_{a,m}|\,\mathsf F_m(\beta_{K''-2i}),
\]
let $\mathrm{JT}(K')$ be the supremum over $K''\geq K'$ of $\sum_{100<i\leq\min(I,K''/2)}\mathsf w_i\Gamma_*(K''-2i)$ plus $G_0(K'')$ if $K''>2I$, and put, for $K\geq a$,
\begin{multline*}
\mathrm{Mix}_a(K)=|f^\natural_{a,0}|\,\bar\Gamma(K-a)+\sum_{m=1}^3|f^\natural_{a,m}|\,\varphi_m(K-a)+\mathrm{HH}_a(K-a)\\
+\Big(\nrm{f_a}_J+\sum_{m\leq3}|f^\natural_{a,m}|\,\mathsf F_m(\beta_a)\Big)\mathrm{JT}(K-a),
\end{multline*}
$\mathrm{Jac}_a(K)=\nrm{f_a}_J\,\bar\Gamma(\max(K-a,0))$, and finally
\[
V_f(K)=\sup_{K'\geq K}\sum_ah_a\min\{\mathrm{Jac}_a(K'),\mathrm{Mix}_a(K')\},
\]
where $\mathrm{Mix}_a(K')$ is omitted for $K'<a$. All these tables are nonincreasing by construction.

\begin{lemma}\label{r5:lem-centre}
For $f\in\{H^\circ,W^\circ_1,Z^\circ,W^\circ\}$, $K\geq0$ and $e\geq0$, $\nrm{A(t^er^KT_K(\xi)f)}_{\mathcal X}\leq R^K\,\Upsilon(K+2e+\deg f)\,V_f(K)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
It suffices to treat one angular component $H_af_a$, with $\Upsilon\equiv1$, and to show that its contribution is at most $R^Kh_a\mathrm{Jac}_a(K)$ and, for $K\geq a$, at most $R^Kh_a\mathrm{Mix}_a(K)$. \emph{Jacobi route.} Expand $r^KT_K=\sum_iC_{K-2i,K}H_{K-2i}t^i$ and multiply by $H_af_a$ with Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-mult}(a),(b): the outputs have angular index at least $K-2i-a$, the lowering steps of the Jacobi parameter that are needed come with enough factors $t$ (as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-mult}(a)), all steps are stochastic, and $\sum_vG_{v'av}h_v\leq h_{v'}h_a$; with Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-conn}(b) this gives $\mathrm{Jac}_a$. \emph{Mixed route}, for $K\geq a$. By $H_a=\sum_nD_{na}t^{(a-n)/2}r^nT_n$ and $T_KT_n=\frac12(T_{K+n}+T_{K-n})$, $t^er^KT_KH_a$ is a nonnegative combination of raw monomials $t^{e'}r^{K'}T_{K'}$ with $K'\geq K-a$ and $R$-weighted mass at most $h_aR^K$. Split $f_a=P_a+(f_a-P_a)$ with $P_a=\sum_{m\leq3}f^\natural_{a,m}(1-t)^m$. The part $P_a$ is bounded by $\bar\Gamma$ for $m=0$ and by Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-raw} for $1\leq m\leq3$. For $f_a-P_a$, in the connection expansion of $t^{e'}r^{K'}T_{K'}$ the components with index $i\leq100$ are bounded with Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-jacobi}(c),(d) term by term in $m>3$, which gives $\mathrm{HH}_a$, and those with $i>100$ by the Jacobi norm, which gives $\mathrm{JT}$: here
\[
\nrm{t^\mu(f_a-P_a)}_{J^{\beta_v},1}\leq\nrm{f_a-P_a}_{J^{\beta_a},1}\leq\nrm{f_a}_J+\sum_{m\leq3}|f^\natural_{a,m}|\,\mathsf F_m(\beta_a),
\]
because every lowering of the Jacobi parameter from $\beta_a$ to $\beta_v<\beta_a$ comes with a factor $t$ (one checks $\mu\geq a-v$ whenever $K\geq a$), and the raising and lowering stencils of Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-jacobi}(b) are nonnegative with column sums one. Finally, $\bar\Gamma$, $\varphi_m$, $\mathrm{HH}_a$ and $\mathrm{JT}$ are nonincreasing and $K'\geq K-a$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{r5:prop-far}
Let $\mathsf p=\Upsilon(1000)/(b\sigma R)$, $N_2=1000$, $k_0=N_2/2$, and for even $N\geq N_*$
\begin{align*}
\mathfrak F(N)=\mathsf p\Big[&\frac1{N+1}\sum_{k=0}^{N/2}(2-\delta_{2k,N})\zeta^kV_{H^\circ}(N-2k)+\frac6R\sum_{k=0}^{N/2-1}\zeta^kV_{W^\circ_1}(N-1-2k)\\
&+3V_{Z^\circ}(N)
+\frac3{2(N+1)}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{N/2}(2-\delta_{2k,N})\zeta^kV_{Z^\circ}(N-2k)\\
&\hphantom{{}+3V_{Z^\circ}(N)+\frac3{2(N+1)}\Big(}+\sum_{k=0}^{N/2-1}(2-\delta_{2k,N-2})\zeta^{k+1}V_{Z^\circ}(N-2-2k)\Big)\\
&+2\rho^2\sum_{(\eta,e)}\gamma_{\eta,e}R^\eta\big(\Phi_P(N_*+\eta,e)+V_{W^\circ}(N_*+\eta)\big)\Big],\\
\mathfrak F_\infty=\mathfrak F(N_2)&+\mathsf p\,\frac{\zeta^{k_0}}{1-\zeta}\Big(\frac{2\,V_{H^\circ}(0)}{N_2+1}+\frac{6\,V_{W^\circ_1}(0)}R+\frac{6\,V_{Z^\circ}(0)}{N_2+1}\Big).
\end{align*}
Then for every odd $j\geq N_*+1$,
\[
\frac{\nrm{(I-AD\Fc(x^\circ))e_j}_{\mathcal X}}{\sigma jR^j\Upsilon(j-1)}\leq Z_{\mathrm{far}}:=\max\big(\{\mathfrak F(N):N_*\leq N\leq N_2\ \text{even}\}\cup\{\mathfrak F_\infty\}\big)+10^{-17}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $j=N+1$. We divide the five parts of \eqref{r5:eq-farcol}, with $\mathsf Me_j$ subtracted from the last, by $b$ and by the input weight $\sigma(N+1)R^{N+1}\Upsilon(N)$. All polynomials involved have degree at most $N+596$ (the degree of $H^\circ$ is at most $596$), so the factor $\Upsilon(1000)$ in $\mathsf p$ covers the grading (Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-transfer}(c)). By $r^NU_N=\sum_k(2-\delta_{2k,N})t^kr^{N-2k}T_{N-2k}$ and Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-centre}, the first part contributes the first sum of $\mathfrak F(N)$; the second, where all Chebyshev coefficients of $U_{N-1}$ equal two, contributes the second sum; for the third, $3N/(N+1)\leq3$ and $r^NU_{N-2}=t\,r^{N-2}U_{N-2}$. For the last two parts, Lemmas~\ref{r5:lem-raw} and~\ref{r5:lem-centre} give the bound with $\Phi_P(N+\eta,e)+V_{W^\circ}(N+\eta)$ in place of the values at $N_*+\eta$.

\emph{Monotonicity in $N$.} The tables $V_f$ are nonincreasing. For $K\geq M+2I+2=442$ the function $K\mapsto\Phi_P(K,e)$ is nonincreasing: the index ranges $i\leq24$, $24<i\leq I$, $i>I$ no longer depend on $K$; $\Gamma_F$ and $\Gamma_H$ are nonincreasing; $\alpha^H(K)=A_t$; the terms of $\mathsf u(K)$ are multiplied by $\zeta$ when $K$ increases by two; the factors $\mathsf g_i=(e+i)/(\beta-i+e+1)$, $\mathsf f_0=e/(\beta+e+1)$, the bound $2/(\beta+e+1)$ for $|\mathsf f'(0)|$, $1/\beta$ and the bound for $|\varrho_2(i)|/i^2$ decrease in $\beta=K+\frac32$; and $G_p(K)$ is nonincreasing, because $i_1$ increases with $K$ and $S_p(i)-\zeta S_p(i+1)=i^p\geq0$. Since $N_*+\eta\geq442$ for $\eta\geq-158$, these terms of $\mathfrak F(N)$ bound the corresponding terms at every $N\geq N_*$. For the monomials with $\eta\leq-160$ and $N>N_*$ we use instead $\nrm{A(t^er^KT_K-\mathcal T_K)}_{\mathcal X}\leq\nrm A\,\nrm{t^er^KT_K-\mathcal T_K}_{\mathcal Y}\leq2\kappa_5\nrm AR^K\Upsilon(K+2e)$, which follows from $t^er^KT_K-\mathcal T_K=\sum_iC_{v_i,K}H_{v_i}(t^{e+i}-1)$ and $\nrm{H_v(t^\mu-1)}_{\mathcal Y}\leq2h_v\Upsilon(v+2\mu)$; the exact total weight $\mathsf p\,2\rho^2\sum_{\eta\leq-160}\gamma_{\eta,e}R^\eta$ of these monomials is below $1.2\cdot10^{-25}$, so their contribution is below $10^{-17}$ for every $N$.

\emph{The $k$-sums.} For $N_*\leq N\leq N_2$ the sums are evaluated exactly. Let $N>N_2$. In the first sum and in the first $Z$-sum the terms with $k<k_0$ have the same weight $2$ as at $N_2$; at $k=k_0$ the weight is $2$ instead of $1$; in the second sum and in the second $Z$-sum, which at $N_2$ end at $k=k_0-1$, the terms with $k\geq k_0$ are new, and in the second $Z$-sum the weight at $k=k_0-1$ is $2$ instead of $1$. All values $V_f(N-2k)$ are at most $V_f(N_2-2k)$ for $k\leq k_0$ and at most $V_f(0)$ in general, and $1/(N+1)\leq1/(N_2+1)$. Hence the excess over the sums evaluated at $N_2$ is at most $\zeta^{k_0}V_f(0)+2\sum_{k>k_0}\zeta^kV_f(0)\leq2\zeta^{k_0}V_f(0)/(1-\zeta)$ for the first sum and for the first $Z$-sum, and at most $2\zeta^{k_0}V_f(0)/(1-\zeta)$ for the second sum and for the second $Z$-sum (where the powers are $\zeta^{k+1}$ with $k\geq k_0-1$). With the prefactors $1/(N_2+1)$, $3/R$ and $3/(2(N_2+1))$ this gives $\mathfrak F_\infty$. Thus every $N>N_2$ is covered by $\mathfrak F_\infty+10^{-17}$. (The bound for $Z_{\mathrm{far}}$ in Table~\ref{r5:tab-bounds} includes the term $10^{-17}$.)
\end{proof}

Collecting all classes,
\[
Z=\max\big\{Z_{\mathrm{fin}},\ Z_{\mathrm{ex}},\ \max_\ell Z_{\mathrm{rad}}(\ell),\ \max_\ell Z_{\mathrm{gap}}(\ell),\ Z_{\mathrm{ang}},\ Z_{\mathrm{sh}},\ Z_{\mathrm{far}}\big\}
\]
bounds $\nrm{I-AD\Fc(x^\circ)}_{\mathcal X\to\mathcal X}$.

%----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Certified bounds, geometry and reconstruction}\label{r5:sec-zero}
%----------------------------------------------------------------------

The bounds in Table~\ref{r5:tab-bounds} are outward roundings of the interval enclosures in the receipt of the verifier (Section~\ref{r5:sec-cap}). The residual uses the entire polynomial support and the fixed dyadic $b$, and all finite columns are computed at the final centre, without transfer from another matrix. The symbol defect is $\eps_\Upsilon<0.025209$, the finite column sums $\delta_e$ are bounded by $Z_{\mathrm{fin}}<1$, and $\rho_{\mathcal B}<2896.07$.

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule
Quantity & Certified bound\\
\midrule
$\nrm A$, $A_t$ & $<104568.72$, $<16.60796$\\
$Y=\nrm{A\Fc(x^\circ)}_{\mathcal X}$ & $<3.19608\cdot10^{-12}$\\
$Z_{\mathrm{fin}}$ ($4698$ columns), $Z_{\mathrm{ex}}$ ($59$ columns) & $<0.0585324$, $<0.496612$\\
field classes: $81$ radial, $47$ angular gap, $Z_{\mathrm{ang}}$ ($\ell\geq256$) & all $<0.887235$; the maximum is $Z_{\mathrm{ang}}$\\

\label{r5:tab-bounds}

\begin{theorem}\label{r5:thm-zero}
There is exactly one zero $x^*=(g^*,c^*)$ of $\Fc$ in $\overline B_{\mathcal X}(x^\circ,10^{-10})$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-A} and the bounds $\eps_\Upsilon<1$, $\max_e\delta_e<1$, the operator $A$ is injective. The classes of Section~\ref{r5:sec-Z} cover all input columns, and $Z<0.887235$. The verifier evaluates both inequalities \eqref{r3:eq-radii} with the full enclosures of $Y$, $Z$, $\nrm A$, $c_2$, $c_3$, $c_4$ at the exact radius $r=10^{-10}$; their left-hand sides are below $-8.0788\cdot10^{-12}$ and $0.887267$, so Proposition~\ref{r3:prop-NK} applies.
\end{proof}

The existence ball projects onto the shape ball $\nrm{c-c^\circ}_{\mathcal E}\leq\eps_0$ with $\eps_0=1044359479949477\cdot10^{-30}\geq r/\sigma$. Let
\[
S_1=\sum_{j\geq3}j|c^\circ_j|,\qquad S_2=\sum_{j\geq3}j(j-1)|c^\circ_j|,\qquad H_{\mathcal E}=\max_{j\geq3\ \mathrm{odd}}\frac{j-1}{R^j\Upsilon(j-1)} ;
\]
the maximum is attained for $j\leq21$, since the ratio of consecutive odd terms is at most $(j+1)/((j-1)R^2)\leq1$ for $j\geq21$.

\begin{proposition}\label{r5:prop-geometry}
For every $c$ with $\nrm{c-c^\circ}_{\mathcal E}\leq\eps_0$: $\re\psi_c'>0.981143$ on $\{|w|\leq\frac{101}{100}\}$; $\re(1+w\psi_c''/\psi_c')>0.839460$ on $\overline\D$; $\re(w\psi_c'/\psi_c)>0.984794$ on $|w|=1$; and $|c_3|>4.45696\cdot10^{-4}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $\delta=c-c^\circ$. For $|w|\leq R$, $|\sum_jj\delta_jw^{j-1}|\leq\nrm\delta_{\mathcal E}/R$, $|\sum_j\delta_jw^j|\leq\nrm\delta_{\mathcal E}/R$ for $|w|\leq1$, and $\sum_jj(j-1)|\delta_j|\leq H_{\mathcal E}\nrm\delta_{\mathcal E}$. Hence $\re\psi_c'\geq c^\circ_1-\sum_{j\geq3}j|c^\circ_j|(\frac{101}{100})^{j-1}-\eps_0/R$ on the disc of radius $\frac{101}{100}$, and on $\overline\D$
\[
\re\Big(1+\frac{w\psi_c''}{\psi_c'}\Big)\geq1-\frac{S_2+\eps_0H_{\mathcal E}}{c^\circ_1-S_1-\eps_0/R},\qquad\re\frac{w\psi_c'}{\psi_c}\geq1-\frac{\sum_{j\geq3}(j-1)|c^\circ_j|+\eps_0/R}{c^\circ_1-\sum_{j\geq3}|c^\circ_j|-\eps_0/R}\quad(|w|=1),
\]
and $|c_3|\geq|c^\circ_3|-\eps_0/(3R^3\Upsilon(2))$. The right-hand sides are evaluated in exact rational arithmetic.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:main} for $n=5$}
Let $x^*=(g^*,c^*)$ be the zero of Theorem~\ref{r5:thm-zero}, and $\psi=\psi_{c^*}$. By Proposition~\ref{r5:prop-geometry}, $\re\psi'>0$ on the disc of radius $\frac{101}{100}$, so by Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-lift}, with $R_0=\frac{101}{100}$, $\Theta=\Theta_{c^*}$ is a real-analytic diffeomorphism of a neighbourhood of $\overline B$ onto its image, with Jacobian $q_{c^*}^3|p_{c^*}|^2>0$. Let $\Omega_0=\Theta(B)$, $U=1+\iota(K_5g^*)$ and $u_0=U\circ\Theta^{-1}$. By Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-F} and Lemma~\ref{r5:lem-lift}(b), $u_0\in C^1(\overline{\Omega_0})$ is a weak solution of $\Delta u_0+\rho^2u_0=0$ with $u_0=1$ and $\nabla u_0=0$ on $\partial\Omega_0$. Let $\Omega=\rho\,\Omega_0$ and $u(X)=u_0(X/\rho)$. Then $u$ is a weak solution of $\Delta u+u=0$ with the same boundary data, and Lemma~\ref{r3:lem-regularity} extends $u$ to a real-analytic solution on a neighbourhood of $\overline\Omega$. The boundary $\partial\Omega=\rho\Theta(\partial B)$ is a compact real-analytic hypersurface diffeomorphic to $S^4$, and $\Omega$ is invariant under $O(4)$ acting on $X'$ and under $X_1\mapsto-X_1$. The lower bound for $\re(w\psi'/\psi)$ makes the meridian domain $\psi(\D)$, and hence $\Omega$, strictly star-shaped with respect to the origin. The lower bound for $\re(1+w\psi''/\psi')$ makes $\psi(\D)$ strictly convex, and the solid-of-revolution argument in the proof of Proposition~\ref{r3:prop-geometry}, with $X'\in\R^4$, shows that $\Omega$ is convex. A constant solution of $\Delta u+u=0$ vanishes, but $u=1$ on $\partial\Omega$. If $\Omega$ were a ball, its symmetries would put its centre at the origin, $\psi(\D)$ would be a disc centred at the origin, and Schwarz's lemma would force $\psi$ to be linear, contrary to $|c^*_3|>4.45696\cdot10^{-4}$. This proves Theorem~\ref{thm:main} for $n=5$.

\begin{remark}\label{r5:rem-ball}
The number $\rho$ agrees with $j_{5/2,17}$ to within $3\cdot10^{-15}$ and $c^\circ_1\approx1.000225$, so $\Omega$ is close to the Schiffer ball of radius $j_{5/2,17}$, which corresponds to $\psi(w)=(j_{5/2,17}/\rho)w$. That ball is far from the existence ball: its coefficient $c_3$ vanishes, so its distance from $x^\circ$ in $\mathcal X$ is at least $3\sigma R^3\Upsilon(2)|c^\circ_3|>148$, while the existence ball has radius $10^{-10}$. The domain $\Omega$ lies at the near-resonance $(j_{5/2,17},12)$ of Table~\ref{tab:resonances}, and its dominant nonconstant zonal harmonic has degree twelve (Remark~\ref{rem:domain}).
\end{remark}
